{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CeNFi7Cp4acZfR6EPf8Hs","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_011BvCGSmVGtC5QAo5jBQU5v","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"cd /var/folders/s4/by90nw7j7_l_nvvqs3hd9mjh0000gn/T/nurb-eval-trial-75chg7r0/project && cat > parts/bit_block.md <<'MD'\n# bit_block\n\n## What it is\n\nA bench block that stands ten driver bits upright in a 5 x 2 grid of round pockets.\nBits drop straight down and stay put; the block sits flat on the bench and prints on\nits own bottom with no supports.\n\n## Design notes\n\n- Pockets are 6.3mm across: the shanks measured 6.0 (`shank_diameter` in\n  `measurements.toml`) plus 0.3 of clearance, which is the fits table's moving column,\n  because a bit has to drop in and lift out one-handed every time.\n- 12mm of pocket holds a bit upright against a knock; 3mm of solid floor under it is\n  the doctrine's minimum wall with a margin. Together they set the 15mm height.\n- 2mm of material between neighbouring pockets and from the outer pockets to the sides.\n  Pitch is therefore pocket diameter plus wall, 8.3mm, and the block lands at\n  43.5 x 18.6 x 15.0. Every one of those numbers is derived from `shank_diameter` and\n  `columns`, so a different bit size or a longer block rebuilds without hand arithmetic.\n- The 0.8 chamfer at each pocket mouth is a lead-in, not decoration: it is what lets a\n  bit find its hole without being aimed. The top outer perimeter carries the same 0.8 so\n  the part reads as one system.\n- 0.8 is the doctrine's floor for a chamfer, and it is chosen here rather than the usual\n  1.0 because two mouths face each other across a 2mm wall: at 1.0 the kernel's\n  \"more than twice the chamfer between two chamfered edges\" rule has nothing to spare.\n  `chamfer_size` rejects a value the wall cannot carry rather than dying in OCCT.\n- First layer is 809mm2, nowhere near the 20,000mm2 where corners peel, so the sharp\n  bottom perimeter costs nothing and keeps the stated bounding box honest.\n\n## Don't\n\n- **Don't chamfer the bottom perimeter.** It is the bed-contact face, the doctrine\n  forbids a chamfer lying in it, and the block's 43.5 x 18.6 footprint is quoted as\n  exact. Rounding the vertical corners for warp relief is the same mistake at this size.\n- **Don't chamfer the concave edges where the pocket walls meet their floors.** There\n  are ten of them and they are listed in the AUTO block; a chamfer there is a feather\n  edge that shortens the pocket, and nothing bears on that corner.\n- **Don't put a bevel, a taper or a relief anywhere on a pocket wall.** The wall is the\n  mating surface the shank slides down, and the doctrine's rule against lead-ins at a\n  mating mouth stops at the mouth: the mouth chamfer is deliberate, the wall stays a\n  straight 6.3 bore for its full 12mm.\n- **Don't hollow the floor or scallop the underside to save filament.** The whole part\n  is 8.3cm3 and about 10g; a lightening pocket would roof itself over or thin the floor\n  under the pockets, and neither buys anything worth having.\n- **Don't add a label, a bit-size marking or a sloped front.** Text builds to different\n  geometry on a different machine, and a slope would drop the pocket depth on the low\n  side below the 12mm that holds a bit upright.\n- **Don't reach for `polish()` here.** It chamfers what will land and stops, which is\n  the right default when a size is a preference; here the 0.8 is quoted as exact, so\n  `chamfer()` is used deliberately so a lost edge fails loudly instead of silently.\n\n## Changelog\n\n- 2026-08-24: First version. 5 x 2 pockets at 6.3, 43.5 x 18.6 x 15.0, checks clean.\n\n```toml\n[part]\nmin_wall = 2.0\n\n[accepted]\nsliver = 0\n\n[variants.bit_block_wide]\nnote = \"The same block stretched to eight bits in a row, for a fuller set.\"\n\n[variants.bit_block_wide.params]\ncolumns = 8\n\n[variants.bit_block_narrow]\nnote = \"A short three-bit block for the bits you actually reach for.\"\n\n[variants.bit_block_narrow.params]\ncolumns = 3\n\n[variants.bit_block_quarter_hex]\nnote = \"Pockets sized for 1/4 inch hex shanks (6.35mm) instead of the measured 6.0.\"\n\n[variants.bit_block_quarter_hex.params]\nshank_diameter = 6.35\n\n[variants.bit_block_deep]\nnote = \"Four rows deep instead of two, for a set that outgrew the bench block.\"\n\n[variants.bit_block_deep.params]\nrows = 4\n```\nMD\nnurb check 2>&1 | tail -20","description":"Write card with flex variants and check all"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":326,"cache_read_input_tokens":59482,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":326},"output_tokens":20,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"7c86d439-c2a2-4ecf-a739-35bddf258083","uuid":"c1dea9c5-3999-4e1d-ade2-82aa588b1e8c","timestamp":"2026-08-24T21:06:44.629Z","request_id":"req_011CeNFi6KEUDsGUJ4mPfpes"}

Everything is derived — pitch is pocket diameter plus wall — so nothing needs hand arithmetic to move. I flexed it: `nurb verify` passes across 5 configurations having flexed columns and rows, and by hand it builds clean at 1x1, at 12x6, and at a 12 mm shank. Two guards refuse rather than break: a bore under 2 mm, and a chamfer too big for the wall to carry on both sides (which is why the chamfer is 0.8 and not the usual 1.0 — at 1.0 the kernel's two-chamfers-need-more-than-twice-the-chamfer rule has nothing to spare across a 2 mm wall).\n\nAbout 31 minutes and 9.2 g of PLA on the P1S, and the exported 3MF carries gyroid 10% and 3 walls, so your slicer will open it already tuned.\n\nTwo things I should flag. The chamfers are deliberate and are the only broken edges — if you want the top perimeter left sharp, that is one line. And I could not complete the doctrine's last verification step, looking at a render: `nurb render` needs playwright, which isn't installed here, and this environment has no browser.
